## Dictionaries

### Mozc Dictionary and Symbol Data

- **Author:** Google Inc. (dictionary entries include data from NAIST, ICOT Free Software, and the Okinawa dictionary)
- **Source:** https://github.com/google/mozc (revision `cbbb6e1bd181cb9f3b409622d916a53a35400ec7`)
- **Files:**
  - `src/data/dictionary_oss/dictionary00.txt` – `dictionary09.txt`
  - `src/data/symbol/symbol.tsv`
  - `src/data/emoji/emoji_data.tsv`
- **Usage:** Converted to `rakukan.dict` binary format and bundled in the installer
- **License:** BSD 3-Clause (Google Inc.). The files under `src/data/dictionary*` are additionally subject to the NAIST / ICOT notice and the Okinawa dictionary public domain statement below. The texts are reproduced from the `LICENSE` file of the Mozc repository at the revision above.

### NVIDIA CUDA Runtime Loader

- **Source:** NVIDIA CUDA Toolkit 13.3 (`lib\x64\cudart_static.lib`). `llama-cpp-sys-2` requests `cudart.lib`; the build script of `rakukan-engine` makes that request resolve to a copy of `cudart_static.lib` (in CUDA 13 both are the same kind of loader library)
- **What is included:** CUDA 13 on Windows uses the "hybrid" CUDA Runtime by default: a small loader library is statically linked into the application, and it loads the CUDA Runtime from the NVIDIA display driver (`nvcudart_hybrid64.dll`). `rakukan_engine_cuda.dll` contains only this loader; it does not import `cudart64_*.dll`, and the CUDA Runtime itself is not bundled (see Not Bundled). See the `--cudart` option in the NVCC documentation: https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-compiler-driver-nvcc/index.html
- **License:** NVIDIA End User License Agreement for the CUDA Toolkit, reproduced below. Attachment A lists the CUDA Runtime files `cudart.dll`, `cudart_static.lib` and `cudadevrt.lib` for Windows as distributable; the loader comes from `cudart_static.lib`.
- **Used in:** `rakukan_engine_cuda.dll` only.

```

Software License Agreement.

Description

The NVIDIA CUDA Toolkit provides command-line and graphical
tools for building, debugging and optimizing the performance
of applications accelerated by NVIDIA GPUs, runtime and math
libraries, and documentation including programming guides,

Default Install Location of CUDA Toolkit

Windows platform:

%ProgramFiles%\NVIDIA GPU Computing Toolkit\CUDA\v#.#

Linux platform:

/usr/local/cuda-#.#

Mac platform:

/Developer/NVIDIA/CUDA-#.#


NVIDIA CUDA Samples


Description

CUDA Samples are now located in
https://github.com/nvidia/cuda-samples, which includes
instructions for obtaining, building, and running the samples.

development environment integrated into Microsoft Visual
Studio that provides tools for debugging, profiling, analyzing
```

---

## Not Bundled

The following are used by rakukan but are not included in the installer. They are governed by their own licenses on the user's system.

- NVIDIA cuBLAS (`cublas64_*.dll`, `cublasLt64_*.dll`) and the NVIDIA display driver (`nvcuda.dll`, and `nvcudart_hybrid64.dll`, which provides the CUDA Runtime), loaded by `rakukan_engine_cuda.dll` (only the CUDA Runtime loader is linked into the DLL; see above)
- Vulkan loader (`vulkan-1.dll`), loaded by `rakukan_engine_vulkan.dll`
- .NET 8 Desktop Runtime, used by the settings app (the settings app is framework-dependent; only the app host above is bundled)
- Conversion models (GGUF, `togatogah/jinen-*` on Hugging Face, licensed under CC-BY-SA-4.0), downloaded from Hugging Face on first use

---

## Source Code of MPL-2.0 Components

The following crate is licensed under the Mozilla Public License 2.0. It is in the dependency graph of the engine DLLs (`rakukan_engine_*.dll`; `hf-hub` → `dirs` → `dirs-sys` → `option-ext`), but on Windows it is not used: `dirs-sys` uses it only in a module compiled for Unix, so no code from it is included in the DLLs. Its source code is listed here as a precaution, unmodified, and is available from:

- `option-ext` 0.2.0: https://crates.io/crates/option-ext/0.2.0 (the published source archive of this exact version; crates.io does not allow a published version to be changed) and https://github.com/soc/option-ext

The license text is in the Rust Crates section below.

```
/// The PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_PUA: [u16; 18] = [
    0xE78D, 0xE78E, 0xE78F, 0xE790, 0xE791, 0xE792, 0xE793, 0xE794, 0xE795, 0xE796, 0xE81E, 0xE826,
    0xE82B, 0xE82C, 0xE832, 0xE843, 0xE854, 0xE864,
];

/// The bytes corresponding to the PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_BYTES: [[u8; 2]; 18] = [
    [0xA6, 0xD9],
    [0xA6, 0xDA],
    [0xA6, 0xDB],
    [0xA6, 0xDC],
    [0xA6, 0xDD],
    [0xA6, 0xDE],
    [0xA6, 0xDF],
    [0xA6, 0xEC],
    [0xA6, 0xED],
    [0xA6, 0xF3],
    [0xFE, 0x59],
    [0xFE, 0x61],
    [0xFE, 0x66],
    [0xFE, 0x67],
    [0xFE, 0x6D],
    [0xFE, 0x7E],
    [0xFE, 0x90],
    [0xFE, 0xA0],
];
```

```
#ifndef OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H
#define OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

#include "../../limbs/limbs.h"

#define P384_LIMBS (384u / LIMB_BITS)

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Z[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT;

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT_AFFINE;
```

```
// CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE.

//! EdDSA Signatures.

use super::ops::ELEM_LEN;
use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize = ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], msg: &[u8]) -> digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx = digest::Context::new(&digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}
```

```
use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature = "alloc")]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize = 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -> Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 >> crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}
```

```
use crate::error::{KeyRejected, Unspecified};

impl From<untrusted::EndOfInput> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<core::array::TryFromSliceError> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: core::array::TryFromSliceError) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<KeyRejected> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: KeyRejected) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}
```
